# Agent 6 — Vendors & Competitors — Findings 2026-08-31

(Method: shared research harness — one agent per competitor, adversarial per-claim verification, completeness gate, DETERMINISTIC table render. 16 competitor(s) targeted, 16 returned signals, 220 verified signals. Confidence capped at Medium per single-agent playbook. Table rows ordered by Printec value (size x win), biddable first.)

## What changed since last run

The biggest structural change is at the top of Printec's own supply chain: the UK CMA formally opened its Phase 1 merger inquiry into Brink's acquisition of NCR Atleos on 26/08/2026, while Atleos' CEO pulled the expected closing date forward to "early in the first quarter of 2027" — and Atleos held no Q2 call and gave no outlook because of it, so Printec's key ATM supplier is now both distracted and about to become a competitor's asset. Second, Brink's Q2 2026 (05/08/2026) confirmed the pivot is real and funded: revenue USD 1,392m (+7% reported, +4% organic), AMS/DRS at 14% organic growth, Europe revenue up from USD 358m to USD 377m, plus a disclosed but unnamed new European ATM outsourcing win — with Brink's Hellas and the Mellon/KAL/Brink's Athens forum putting that pressure directly in Printec's home market. Third, the Geldservice Austria EUR 9.1m combined deposit-and-withdrawal recycler framework (TED 305531-2026, published 05/05/2026) is still unawarded against a Diebold Nixdorf incumbency — the single largest open contest in the footprint. Fourth, Mellon has audited scale for the first time (FY2025 group revenue EUR 258.82m, +17%) and is expanding by entity into Slovenia, Hungary and Croatia, while Diebold's Banking product sales fell 13.0% YoY and Worldline's Financial Services division shrank again — the regional peer is gaining where the global OEMs are losing. Verification corrected or could not confirm several baselines: the PrivatBank recycling-ATM programme status and the Newland POS award attribution came back contradicted; the Auro Domus ">600 ATMs in Croatia" baseline is not supported by the company's own material; Euronet's ATM estate growth, the Hungarian small-settlement ATM mandate read, and the Worldline headcount/MeTS items were contradicted as filed; and the Payten-inside-NBS claim is withdrawn as unsourced. The Euronet/CrediaBank Greek acquiring plus 141-ATM management deal is confirmed as still not closed as at 31/08/2026, and no Glory award has surfaced at PrivatBank.

## Top opportunities (ranked)

1. Geldservice Austria — cash recycler supply and lifecycle service against the Diebold incumbent on the EUR 9.1m framework (XL, Medium)
2. Brink's Hellas / Greek banks — ATM managed services and recycling fleet defence ahead of the Brink's-Atleos close (XL, Medium)
3. Epirus Bank and mid-tier Greek banks — counter-offer to Atleos ATMaaS with Printec-managed ATM services (L, Medium)
4. Sens Bank Ukraine — self-service estate service contract UA-2026-08-19-010667-a, UAH 29.8m, live in qualification (M, Medium)
5. Sense Bank Ukraine — POS post-warranty service, three lots, UAH 18.4m, bids closing 01/09/2026 (M, Medium)
6. Euro-area footprint banks (AT, GR, CY, HR, SI, SK) — recycler and sorter refresh tied to the ECB euro banknote redesign Glory is already selling into (L, Medium)
7. ELTA-type Greek public buyers — counter BOOS S.A. on banknote counting/sorting operational lease deals in the home market (M, Medium)
8. Sens Bank Ukraine — branch and ATM physical-security maintenance, twice-failed at UAH 722.5k and still unplaced (S, Medium)

| Glory Ltd — ECB euro banknote redesign as a stated demand driver | Austria, Croatia, Cyprus, Greece, Slovakia, Slovenia (euro-area footprint) | In its 07/08/2026 investor conference material Glory devotes a slide to the 'ECB Euro Project' (described as a complete redesign of euro banknotes running in parallel with the digital-euro plan) and tells investors that on 23/07/2026 the ECB released 10 candidate designs and opened a public vote. Glory's stated read: cash usage 'is expected to remain firmly established in Europe', cash-infrastructure updates will therefore continue, and 'special demand related to banknote redesign is expected from around 2029' — issued in stages, so it may run for several years. This is an OEM putting a dated, multi-year hardware-refresh cycle into its own investor guidance. The ECB's own 23/07/2026 press release confirms the mechanics and adds firm dates the Glory slide does not: ten shortlisted proposals on the themes 'European culture' and 'Rivers and birds', a public online survey open until 21/09/2026, and a Governing Council decision on the final design expected around the end of 2026, after which development and production begin — the ECB says it will be several years before the notes reach circulation. In plain terms: when a banknote series is redesigned, every machine that authenticates or sorts notes — teller cash recyclers (TCRs), banknote sorters, ATM deposit and recycling modules, retail cash recyclers — needs new recognition sensors, new note templates or replacement, and re-testing against the new series. Because a series change hits recognition software and sensors more than whole machines, it should be mostly a service-and-upgrade annuity — exactly where Printec's field organisation is strong and a pure OEM is weak. Printec should open the conversation now with euro-area central banks and the large branch networks (Alpha Bank, Erste Croatia, Bank of Cyprus and peers) about a phased recognition-upgrade programme, so budget lines are shaped in 2027–2028 rather than arriving as a single OEM package around 2029. | ECB public survey closes 21/09/2026 and the Governing Council selects a design around end-2026; procurement shaping 2027–2028, Glory guides equipment demand from around 2029 | Medium | L | Medium | Watch for the ECB's report on the survey results and the Governing Council design decision expected around end-2026, then the published issuance timetable; open an upgrade-programme dialogue with Bank of Greece, Central Bank of Cyprus, Banka Slovenije, Národná banka Slovenska, HNB and OeNB, and with euro-area commercial branch networks, during 2027 budget planning. |

| Albanian ATM cash-automation market, including the non-bank ATM partnership operators (independent ATM deployer layer) | Albania | [market trigger — the deposit-automation curve Printec's NCR line is built for] Bank of Albania statistics show automated CASH DEPOSITS at ATMs in Albania reached 676,000 in Q1 2026, up 125% year on year, worth ALL 33.4bn, up 146%. For full-year 2025 there were nearly 2.2 million ATM cash deposits, up 109%, worth ALL 104.8bn, up ~110%. The installed base is following: ATMs capable of accepting cash deposits rose to 454 at end-March 2026, from 430 at end-2025 and 408 a year earlier — about 40% of the country's ATMs. The total active ATM estate was 1,147 at end-March 2026, up 8% year on year. The structurally important detail, and it is sharper than previously reported: recent ATM growth has been driven especially by machines placed in TOURIST AREAS, and these are typically NOT operated directly by the banks but under a partnership model with third-party businesses which, in the source's own words, are usually THE COMPANIES THAT SELL ATM EQUIPMENT. In that split the bank supplies the payments infrastructure — the system, the accounting and the cash — while the partner supplies the ATM itself and carries the operating costs (transport, replenishment, cash insurance) and the site-rental relationships with the host businesses, with revenue shared according to each side's costs. An independent-ATM-deployer (IAD) layer is therefore forming in Albania alongside the bank estates, and it is being formed by hardware vendors. [partially-confirmed] | [Banka e Shqipërisë statistics, reported by Monitor.al (re-fetched via WordPress REST API 31/08/2026)](https://monitor.al/depozitimet-e-parave-ne-atm-u-rriten-me-125-ne-tremujorin-e-pare-te-vitit/) | 29/07/2026 | Deposit volumes more than doubling while only about 40% of the estate can take a deposit is the clearest hardware-replacement case on this beat, and it maps directly onto Printec's NCR intelligent-deposit and recycling ATMs plus NCR APTRA OptiCash for cash optimisation — when deposits surge, cash forecasting and branch cash cycles become the bank's real cost problem. The IAD point is now a sharper competitive warning than first written: the partners running the fastest-growing slice of the Albanian estate are themselves ATM equipment sellers, which makes them simultaneously Printec's most direct rivals and its most natural channel. Printec should identify and approach those operators directly with hardware plus managed field service and telemetry, rather than working Albania only through the banks, or it will cede the growth segment to whoever is already supplying and servicing those machines. | Deposit-capable ATM replacement and expansion continues over the next 6-18 months | Medium | L | Medium | Name the third-party businesses operating Albania's tourist-area ATMs — the source says they are usually ATM equipment vendors, so this is a competitor-identification task as much as a prospect list — and identify which OEM's deposit modules the 454 deposit-capable machines use, which tells Printec whether this is an NCR upgrade path or a competitor displacement. |

| Romanian card-acceptance mandate and the bank in-house softPOS response (ING, OTP, Alpha Bank, CEC Bank, BCR) | Romania | market-structure context, not a fresh trigger. [STANDING - in force since 01/01/2026] Romania's card-acceptance obligation was widened with effect from 01/01/2026 through amendments to OUG 193/2002 (read together with Law 265/2022 on the Trade Register), obliging entities registered in the Trade Register to accept modern electronic payment means, with ANAF enforcement. Reported penalties are RON 5,000-7,500 for refusing to accept a modern payment means and RON 20,000-50,000 for breaching the general obligation, and entities that collect exclusively through credit-institution accounts are exempt; accepting institutions must install a terminal within 30 days of a request. Romanian banks have largely responded with software rather than hardware, shipping their own softPOS applications (softPOS = an app turning an ordinary NFC smartphone into a card-acceptance terminal) - reported for ING, OTP (POSibil), Alpha Bank and CEC Bank, and confirmed for BCR through the Global Payments GP tom app. The specific amending-act number, the alleged removal of a prior turnover threshold, and the claim that a virtual POS or mobile app satisfies the obligation are NOT established and are excluded here. On 13/01/2026 Atleos announced that Epirus Bank selected it to modernise and expand the bank's ATM network across Greece under the ATMaaS model, using Cashzone network resources 'to provide a branded retail network'. Atleos takes ATM network lifecycle services including location selection, installation, maintenance and operational support; the ATMs 'are expected to feature Epirus Bank and Cashzone co-branding'. Deployment began November 2025 with phased completion targeted 'within approximately three months'. No unit count and no contract value were disclosed. The release is quoted by Anthony Parks, VP Europe and Middle East Sales for Atleos, and the accompanying image shows a co-branded ATM at Syntagma metro station in Athens; the Epirus Bank CEO frames it as part of 'a nationwide expansion across Greece'. Cashzone is Atleos' own independent ATM deployer (IAD) brand — an operator that owns and runs ATMs in retail locations rather than for a bank. Greece is a live Cashzone market: the Greek-language site cashzone.gr states 'more than 20,000 ATMs across Europe', '285,000 ATMs in 14 countries' and '2.6 billion transactions a year'. | Euronet Worldwide — ATM network cash and risk transfer | Group / Europe | [STANDING — dated 2026, recurring quarterly disclosure] Euronet's contracts with financial institutions in the Payments Infrastructure segment make Euronet responsible for damage to ATMs and theft of ATM network cash. This is a seasonal peak, not a run-rate: the same disclosure in the FY2025 10-K put the balance at USD 460.3 million at 31/12/2025, and on the Q2 call the CFO described 'nearly $1 billion in cash deployed in our ATM network' as 'seasonal funding required to support peak ATM cash demand'. The disclosure is recurring boilerplate in every Euronet periodic filing, not a new development — its value is as evidence of the commercial reason banks find ATM-as-a-Service attractive: the outsourcer absorbs the cash, insurance and cash-in-transit (CIT, the armoured-vehicle cash replenishment service) risk, not just the hardware. | Euronet Card Services Greece — ATM surcharge regime under law 5222/2025 | Greece | [STANDING — dated 2025] Article 271 of Greek law 5222/2025 amended article 48 of law 5167/2024 and tightened the rules on third-party ATM withdrawals in two ways. First, it sets a national cap of EUR 1.50 on the charge a legal entity operating ATMs (i.e. a non-bank operator) may levy on a cardholder. Second — and this is the opposite of an exemption — it imposes a SPECIAL ZERO-CHARGE requirement where the cardholder's payment service provider has a direct or indirect stake in the management or capital of the ATM-operating company; the Piraeus Bank / Cashflex relationship is the case that prompted the government reaction and the amendment. In the CrediaBank/Euronet context, CrediaBank told Greek press there is no administrative or shareholding relationship with Euronet, so the fee-free access CrediaBank customers get across Euronet's 2,500-plus Greek ATM network rests purely on a bilateral commercial agreement, not on the statutory zero-charge rule. Euronet has been present in Greece for more than 25 years across all its divisions and has been rolling out a marketed 'free-of-charge' ATM network via retail-site agreements since 2023-2024. Cashflex (Piraeus-linked, local-peer) is the other bank-affiliated Greek ATM operator this regime bears on. | Central Bank of Montenegro (CBCG) TIPS Clone go-live and the EU Verification-of-Payee mandate | Montenegro | [market/regulatory trigger — NOW CONFIRMED AS EXECUTED, NOT PLANNED] The Central Bank of Montenegro's own website states that on 20 JULY 2026 the CBCG introduced EU instant payments into domestic payment traffic through the TIPS Clone system, in cooperation with the Bank of Italy, under the auspices of the European Central Bank and with World Bank support. TIPS Clone is a national instant-payments settlement platform cloned from the ECB's TARGET Instant Payment Settlement service: money moves account-to-account in seconds, 24 hours a day, every day including weekends and holidays. The enabling amendments to the Law on Payment Transactions (reported 07/07/2026 while still in parliamentary procedure) oblige every bank and payment service provider offering standard credit transfers to also offer instant execution across all electronic channels customers already use, with funds available to the payee within TEN SECONDS of receipt of the order and an obligation on the payer's bank to return the funds without delay if it does not receive confirmation inside that window. In line with EU Regulation (EU) 2024/886 the law introduces MANDATORY VERIFICATION OF PAYEE (VoP) — the bank must check before confirming the order that the payee's name matches the IBAN entered, must warn the user on a mismatch, and must provide the check FREE OF CHARGE; banks face fines of EUR 10,000 to EUR 40,000 for non-compliance. The law also lets domestic payment institutions and e-money institutions participate directly in settlement-final payment systems, adds sanctions-screening obligations, and aligns Montenegro with EU digital operational resilience rules. Montenegro joined SEPA the previous year. | [Central Bank of Montenegro, 'Instant plaćanja (TIPS Clone)' official page (re-fetched and read 31/08/2026); draft Law on Payment Transactions as reported by biznis.rs / eKapija 07/07/2026](https://cbcg.me/me/kljucne-funkcije/platni-promet/instant-placanja-tips-clone) | 20/07/2026 | Every Montenegrin bank now has a live, legally compelled requirement to run real-time payee-name matching and to settle irrevocably inside ten seconds, and the ten-second window makes after-the-fact fraud review useless, so screening has to move inline. That is a dated, concrete opening for Printec's transaction-monitoring line (INETCO) and its AML and fraud screening line (FICO), plus digital onboarding and eKYC to keep the name-matching data clean at source. Montenegro is a small market where every bank faces the same deadline at the same time, which is the ideal shape for a single packaged offer sold repeatedly. Printec should build a VoP-plus-inline-monitoring package for Montenegro now and hold it ready to reuse in Albania and Kosovo when their equivalents land. | System live since 20/07/2026; bank-side VoP and monitoring build-out over the next 6-12 months | Medium | M | Medium | Identify which vendor supplied the VoP name-matching engine to the first Montenegrin banks — that vendor becomes the regional incumbent. | Czech EET 2.0 fiscal-POS cycle (Ministry of Finance / Finanční správa; Dotypay already in roster) | Czechia | [regulatory-driven cycle — now verified against a Czech GOVERNMENT source, and materially different from the vendor's framing] The Chamber of Deputies approved the government bill on sales recording, 'EET 2.0', in third reading on 15/07/2026; it then went to the Senate. Finanční správa published its technical documentation for DEVELOPERS AND SUPPLIERS OF CASH-REGISTER SYSTEMS on 05/06/2026 and launched eet.gov.cz, with a firm published timetable: 05/06/2026 technical documentation, 23/06/2026 developer seminar in Prague, 01/07/2026 'Playground' test environment, 01/11/2026 EET 2.0 functions in the DIS+ tax mailbox on the MOJE daně portal including generation of cash-register certificates, 01/12/2026 launch of the free MOJE EET app for small businesses, 01/01/2027 start of operation with a pilot regime that month, and 01/02/2027 full live operation. The scheme records ONLY contact payments — cash, cards, QR and similar at the point of sale — and excludes remote payments such as bank transfers and invoices. CRITICALLY, the Ministry of Finance states the law 'nezavádí povinný tisk účtenek ani nevyžaduje pořizování nových pokladních zařízení' — it introduces no mandatory receipt printing and DOES NOT REQUIRE THE PURCHASE OF NEW CASH-REGISTER EQUIPMENT — and the state will give the smallest traders a free web application usable on a phone, tablet or computer. MoF estimates CZK 14.4bn a year in additional revenue; the package also cuts VAT on non-alcoholic drinks in food service to 12% and exempts voluntary tips up to 7% of turnover. Vendors are already selling against the deadline: Dotypay published an all-in-one payment-terminal-plus-cash-register campaign page on 17/07/2026 offering free terminal rental, a built-in printer, a free data SIM and automatic remote software updates for EET 2.0 compliance. [partially-confirmed] | [Finanční správa ČR / Ministerstvo financí — 'Finanční správa zahajuje technickou přípravu k EET 2.0' (05/06/2026) and 'Sněmovna schválila EET 2.0' (15/07/2026)](https://www.financnisprava.cz/cs/financni-sprava/media-a-verejnost/tiskove-zpravy-gfr/tiskove-zpravy-2026/financni-sprava-zahajuje-technickou-pripravu-k-eet) | 05/06/2026 | Czechia gets a nationwide, simultaneous fiscal-recording mandate from 01/01/2027, but it is NOT the mandated hardware refresh the vendor page implies — the Ministry of Finance explicitly says no new cash-register equipment is required and is shipping a free state app for the smallest traders. So the money is not in re-terminalling the whole base; it is in software compliance, certificate handling and remote fleet update. Printec Czechia's play is therefore terminal management (TMS) and over-the-air update capability sold to acquirers and ISOs whose existing estates must be made compliant, plus integration work against the published Finanční správa specification — the 01/11/2026 cash-register-certificate milestone is the concrete integration date. There is still a real replacement upsell where merchants choose to modernise, which is exactly the wedge Dotypay is using with free hardware, so Printec must be able to match on remote update rather than on box price. | High — bill passed the Chamber on 15/07/2026 and the tax authority is executing a published technical timetable, though Senate passage and signature were not confirmed in this check | Medium | M | Medium | Confirm the bill completed its Senate passage and was signed — as of the sources re-checked it had only cleared the Chamber of Deputies. | Euronet Magyarorszag — Community ATM programme vs the small-settlement ATM mandate | Hungary | [STANDING — dated 2025, with a live end-2026 deadline] A Hungarian government decree published in Magyar Kozlony on 29/05/2025 and in force from 07/06/2025 requires an ATM to be operating in every settlement of at least 1,000 inhabitants by end-2025 and in every settlement of 500 to 1,000 inhabitants by END-2026; smaller places are excluded for now. The decree makes banks responsible for installing the machines and municipalities responsible for designating a suitable site — accessible, in a busy location, near everyday administrative services, step-free, well lit, with reliable power and internet, and physically secure enough to be serviced and replenished. Where no compliant site exists, the municipality and the bank must jointly look for alternatives. Euronet, historically concentrated in Hungarian city-centre and tourist locations, markets a 'Kozossegi ATM' (Community ATM) programme to municipalities under which the municipality supplies a site free of charge and Euronet takes full responsibility for installing and operating the machine. | Central Bank of Cyprus (licensing regime for payment and e-money institutions) | Cyprus | [regulator] The Central Bank of Cyprus announced on Friday 31/07/2026 changes to the licensing process for Electronic Money Institutions and Payment Institutions, with effect from 03/08/2026, as part of the licensing and supervisory strategy it first announced on 20/12/2024. The changes, quoted from the CBC's own announcement: a new 'pre-application exploratory stage' before a formal application, at which interested entities must submit specified information and pay a NON-REFUNDABLE fee of EUR 5,000; the fee for submitting a licensing application increased from EUR 5,000 to EUR 10,000; a CBC power to require, where deemed necessary, an enhanced due-diligence report prepared by a firm with relevant expertise, either during the pre-application stage or during assessment of an application; and inactive applications being deemed withdrawn after a predefined period of non-response to CBC comments or requests. The CBC states the exploratory stage has a twofold objective — improving applicants' understanding of its requirements, and early identification of issues affecting the subsequent assessment, particularly good repute. A separate 2026 Directive on the internal organisation and governance of electronic money institutions was reported in the press as entering into force on 29/05/2026; that directive is NOT referenced in this CBC announcement and was not located in the CBC's announcements list, so it should be treated as unconfirmed until the CBC directives register is checked. | [Central Bank of Cyprus — announcement 'New Licensing Process for Electronic Money Institutions and Payment Institutions'](https://www.centralbank.cy/en/announcements/new-licensing-process-31-07-2026) | 31/07/2026 | This changes the shape of Printec's Cyprus competitive set rather than any single vendor. Raising the cost and scrutiny of a Cyprus PI/EMI licence — a EUR 15,000 combined cash cost before a single application is assessed, plus a possible externally-prepared due-diligence report — slows the arrival of new small acquirers and softPOS entrants, the class of competitor that has been eroding terminal economics, and pushes value toward the already-licensed incumbents (JCC, payabl., Viva.com). For Printec the practical read is that the Cyprus acceptance market will consolidate around a knowable, finite set of licensed players, so account planning there can be exhaustive rather than defensive, and the licensed EMIs themselves become a target list for AML, KYC and transaction-monitoring work as CBC scrutiny of governance and good repute rises. | In force since 03/08/2026; EMI compliance and governance remediation demand over the next 6-12 months | Medium | S | Medium | Build a target list of currently licensed Cypriot EMIs and PIs from the CBC's official registers and pitch AML and transaction-monitoring remediation against the raised good-repute and due-diligence bar. ## Verification notes
- **Corrected/contradicted by the adversarial pass (24):** PrivatBank recycling-ATM programme (PB-2026-03-17-3068329) — Portal JSON re-fetched and polled 31/08/2026; status active.stage2.waiting confirmed. THREE CORRECTIONS. (1) FABRICATED FIGURE REMOVED: the researcher titled this an '800-unit recycling-ATM programme'. There is no unit count anywhere in the record - the only '800' in the document is a height specification ('картоприймач, NFC та клавіатура має бути розміщено на висоті 800-1100 мм'). The record's value field is 0 and both item lines carry quantity 1 as spec placeholders. The 800-unit claim has been struck from the entity name and signal text. (2) TIMES CORRECTED: the qualification period decodes in Kyiv time as 08/04/2026 15:00 to 25/08/2026 08:48, not '08/04/2026 12:00' to '25/08/2026 05:48' - the same UTC-as-local error as the sorter row. (3) OEM FRAMING CONTRADICTED: the researcher's implies told Printec to position 'NCR recycling-ATM line and x-core software'. The published specification requires Vynamic View, ProFlex 4.x / VCP Pro 7 and CEN/XFS 3.50 - Diebold Nixdorf software - so the NCR-led pitch is wrong and the implies has been rewritten. The related backstory claim that Printec 'supplied the incumbent 1,140 NCR recycling ATMs' is a carry-over from a previous cycle that I could not independently confirm in any primary source, so it has been removed from the signal. Win downgraded Medium to Low on the DN specification lock.; EAE Solutions B.V. (Netherlands) and Isitec International / ISITEC SYSTEM ROMANIA SRL (France/Romania) — MATERIALLY CONTRADICTED AND REWRITTEN. The original claim that EAE Solutions won a contract 'covering BOTH Posta Slovenije and C.N. Posta Romana' is FALSE. I pulled the notice directly: TED 467778-2026 (https://ted.europa.eu/en/notice/467778-2026/html) shows buyer POSTA SLOVENIJE d.o.o. and NUTS SI032 Podravska only, scope a single parcel sorter at PLC Maribor with five-year maintenance; the EUR 6,772,535 value, the EUR 6,825,000 estimate and the 11/06/2026 conclusion date are all correct but belong entirely to Slovenia. A TED full-text query for 'Posta Romana' AND 'EAE' returns ZERO notices. The likely origin of the error is EAE's OTHER 2026 win, TED 351992-2026, where the buyer is AN POST (Ireland) - almost certainly misread as Posta Romana. ALSO CORRECTED: the claimed Isitec figure of 'RON 12,425,000 [STANDING - 15/05/2025]' could not be reproduced; TED shows RON 2,490,195 on the 41463-2026 award and RON 2,665,000 per lot on the 15/05/2025 contract being modified. INDEPENDENTLY CONFIRMED AND ADDED: Printec S.I. d.o.o.'s Posta Slovenije win (TED 624355-2025, EUR 3,594,900, concluded 14/08/2025), which the original row asserted without a citation; and two fresh C.N. Posta Romana contract-modification notices dispatched 03/08/2026 and 10/08/2026, which give this row a genuine in-window trigger it previously lacked. Isitec's product range and its listing of Romania among its operating countries were separately confirmed on isitec-international.com.; Romanian card-acceptance mandate and the bank in-house softPOS response (ING, OTP, Alpha Bank, CEC Bank, BCR) — CORRECTED ON MULTIPLE POINTS after re-fetching the StartupCafe analysis. (1) DATE: the article is dated 16/02/2026, not 01/01/2026 - source_date fixed. (2) LEGAL REFERENCE: the article cites OUG 193/2002 as amended with effect from 01/01/2026, together with Law 265/2022; it does NOT cite 'OUG/Legea 239/2025', so that reference is removed as unverified. (3) FINES: the row gave a single RON 20,000-50,000 band; the article actually gives TWO - RON 5,000-7,500 for refusing to accept a modern payment means and RON 20,000-50,000 for breaching the general obligation - both now stated. (4) THRESHOLD: the article does NOT mention removal of a 'RON 50,000 turnover threshold'; that claim is unsupported and additionally the historic OUG 193/2002 threshold is commonly cited in EUR rather than RON, so both the figure and its currency are in doubt - claim removed. (5) VIRTUAL POS: the article does not state that a virtual POS or mobile app satisfies the obligation - claim removed; it does state accepting institutions must install a terminal within 30 days of request, and that entities collecting exclusively through credit-institution accounts are exempt, both of which are now included. (6) The ING, OTP POSibil, Alpha Bank and CEC Bank softPOS launches are asserted without an individual source and are flagged as reported only; the BCR/GP tom leg is separately verified. is_new set to false: this is standing legal context in force since January 2026 with no new trigger. Confidence stays Low.; Worldline SA (global) — Western European headcount reduction — CORRECTED FIGURE. The researcher claimed 'The CEO has guided to a headcount reduction of 6-7% per year in Western Europe, with total group headcount expected to fall around 30%', sourced to a MarketScreener report. The primary H1 2026 press release says the opposite number in plain terms: 'Les effectifs en Europe occidentale ont diminue de 3 %, conformement au rythme annuel de 4 a 5 % annonce lors du Capital Markets Day pour la duree du plan North Star 2030' — a guided annual rate of 4 to 5%, not 6 to 7%. I also read the CMD press release (pr-2025_11_06_01) and the FY2025 results release (pr-2026_02_25_02) and neither contains a 6-7% figure or a ~30% total-headcount-reduction figure, and the H1 earnings call transcript gives no percentage at all (the CEO says only 'reduction of headcount in Western Europe ahead of our 2030 trajectory'). The 6-7% and the -30% are therefore aggregator-only and unreproducible; both have been removed and replaced with the guided 4-5%. The verifiable primary evidence — 3% H1 reduction, EUR 12m / -1.9% personnel-cost saving, 66% internal mobility, 60% cut in restructuring cost — is retained and is enough to carry the Printec argument on its own. Slide citations also fixed: the FTE and mobility data are on slide 10, not slide 13.; Euronet Card Services Greece — ATM surcharge regime under law 5222/2025 — I re-read the cited financialreport.gr article in the original Greek and the researcher inverted the statute. The Greek text reads: 'προβλέπεται εθνικό πλαφόν 1,50 ευρώ για αναλήψεις από ΑΤΜ επιχειρήσεων-νομικών οντοτήτων που εκμεταλλεύονται ΑΤΜ (δηλαδή τρίτων) και ειδική πρόβλεψη ΜΗΔΕΝΙΚΗΣ ΧΡΕΩΣΗΣ όταν υπάρχει άμεση ή έμμεση συμμετοχή του παρόχου υπηρεσιών πληρωμών στη διοίκηση ή στο κεφάλαιο της επιχείρησης που εκμεταλλεύεται το ΑΤΜ (όπως η σχέση Τράπεζας Πειραιώς-Cashflex)'. That is a special provision of ZERO charge for stake-linked operators — a stricter obligation — not, as the signal claimed, an exemption from the cap. Using the researcher's version in a bank meeting would have been an own goal. CORRECTED above, and the Piraeus/Cashflex example added. Also corrected: the instrument is article 271 of law 5222/2025 amending article 48 of law 5167/2024, which the researcher did not specify. CONFIRMED unchanged: the EUR 1.50 cap; CrediaBank's statement that it has no administrative or shareholding relationship with Euronet; the 2,500+ fee-free Euronet ATM network; and 'more than 25 years' in Greece (also confirmed verbatim in Euronet's own 07/01/2026 release). DROPPED per the no-invented-numbers rule: the 'more than 2,200 off-branch ATMs' figure, which I could find in no primary or press source I could reach. COMPANY LIMITED — The Newland award itself is CONFIRMED exactly on the OpenProcurement central database: USD 2,180,000 excluding VAT, award active 11/06/2026, 20,000 units of 'Переносний платіжний POS-термінал Newland ... модель N950', procurement method 'reporting'. Everything the researcher attached to it is wrong or unverifiable. (1) FIGURES NOT FOUND: the claim that 'PrivatBank awarded two 2026 Android POS lots totalling USD 9,025,000 excluding VAT, with the winner of the larger USD 7,125,000 lot still unpublished' matches no record. In fact PRINTEC UKRAINE LLC, EDRPOU 35442628, won the larger stationary lot - 39,000 Castles Saturn 1000 S1F3CT terminals with HAL API, UAH 151,734,960 including VAT, award active 25/10/2025 on UA-2025-10-25-000676-a. The TMS pitch has been removed from the implies and turned into a verification task in followup. keep=true because the underlying award is solid and the corrected picture is more favourable to Printec, not less.; Euronet Worldwide — ATM estate growth and PI margins — Two material errors, both corrected above. Correct Q2 comparison is revenue +11% against direct costs +10%. (2) CAUSATION: 'real margin compression on the ATM/outsourcing base' is contradicted by both primaries. PI gross margin ROSE to 49.4% from 48.6% (10-Q, Gross profit paragraph), and the 8-K EX-99.1 of 30/07/2026 (https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1029199/000155485526001642/ex991_1.htm) attributes the 2% operating-income growth to ~USD 4.7m of extra non-cash CoreCard purchase-price amortisation, stating ex-that it would have grown 7%. UNCONFIRMED and REMOVED per the no-invented-numbers rule: the '1,039 settlements identified as having no ATM' figure, the claim that the MNB allocates named settlements to named banks, the claim that the law expressly permits banks to install and operate ATMs jointly, and the 'roughly 600 ATMs in Hungary' estate figure — none appears in the source and vendors do not publish per-country unit counts. The uncited G+D claims about the Bulgarian National Bank (EUR 181,000), the National Bank of Ukraine (EUR 44,062.26 / 1,214 spare parts) and Státní tiskárna cenin (EUR 216,000, 28/07/2026) carried no source and could not be re-checked; they have been removed. Replacement evidence found and verified: TED 856282-2025, Geldservice Austria to Giesecke+Devrient Currency Technology GmbH, EUR 615,135.40, procedure neg-wo-call, one tender, published 23/12/2025.; Global Payments Europe, s.r.o. at Statna pokladnica (Alanata, CGI, VNET and HICO claims unsupported) — The cited URL supports only the Global Payments Europe / Statna pokladnica card-acceptance contract of 03/02/2020. All four 2026-dated claims bundled into this signal (Alanata at NBS, CGI Slovakia modification 13/08/2026, VNET DDoS, HICO construction supervision) are absent from the source and unverified. The source_date of 13/08/2026 is not supported; corrected to the document's actual publication date, which also makes this stale context rather than news.
- **Unconfirmed (single/weak source, kept at Low) (10):** OFFICE HQ SRL (RO32152007), CA.DA GRUPP and Zoopie Solutions (RO45414878), MASTER TEAM DOO (PIB 100093505), Info-Kod Rešenja d.o.o. (PIB 106780962) and Payten d.o.o. (PIB 110428879) — terminal supply at the National Bank of Serbia, exceet Card Schweiz AG (Urdorf, Switzerland), LATIS DOO BEOGRAD (PIB 103748438), TAURUNUM OBEZBEDJENJE DOO (PIB 109012058) and TAURUNUM OBEZBEDJENJE PLUS DOO, EUTRON INVEST ROMANIA SRL, MONTRAN SRL (Romania), BUSLOGIC BUS SYSTEM DOO POZAREVAC (PIB 114633193), Wall Street Systems Sweden AB (ION Group)

| NCR Atleos (Printec PARTNER) | 1) SEC EDGAR Archives returned HTTP 403 to WebFetch on the Q2 EX-99.1 earnings release URL. Resolved per the access protocol by retrying the SAME primary URL with curl and a desktop Chrome User-Agent plus a contact e-mail (SEC requires an identifying UA); the release and the 10-Q were then extracted and de-tagged locally. Nothing was dropped. 2) First 10-Q fetch attempt used the 8-K accession folder (0001974138-26-000014) and returned a 217-byte stub; resolved by reading the EDGAR submissions JSON (data.sec.gov/submissions/CIK0001974138.json) to get the correct 10-Q accession 0001974138-26-000016. 3) fintechfutures.com returned HTTP 403 to WebFetch on the Lloyds/Atleos article; not escalated further because the item proved to be July 2025, non-footprint and out of scope, and the substance was available from the Atleos investor release. 4) comesacompetition.org served the case-registry page but the underlying Website-Notice-Brink-NCR.pdf carries no dates in the rendered page; not escalated, non-footprint jurisdiction. 5) cashzone.gr/en/about is pure marketing copy and publishes NO ATM count, partner-bank list or launch date - the Greek Cashzone estate size remains unquantified and I have not estimated it. 6) No paywall blocked anything material this run; the MLex article on the CMA probe is subscription-only but the CMA case page itself is the better primary source and was fully readable. |

No fallback exists inside the agent — WebFetch cannot discover URLs it has not been given — so the app-store, lost-deal-press and vendor-case-study angles were dropped entirely and are logged here rather than silently omitted. 2) intel-cache/prozorro_notices.json cannot yield winner names by script, by design: the Prozorro public search omits the tender UUID, there is no public friendly-ID->UUID resolver, and the CDB at public.api.openprocurement.org requires the UUID. Not attempted this run; the Ukrainian rows rest on Agent 2's already-resolved pulls. 3) Carried forward unresolved from Agent 2 (27/08/2026), all still blocking and all relevant to this unit: Greece KIMDIS/ESIDIS returns HTTP 503 (WebLogic 'no backend server available'); Bulgaria app.eop.bg serves only a 7,500-byte Angular shell behind an F5/BIG-IP WAF on every route including /api/v1/public/*; Serbia jnportal.ujn.gov.rs/api/* returns 401/403 without a session cookie plus UserToken header; Croatia eojn.hr and Slovenia enarocanje.si are SPA shells that a desktop User-Agent alone does not defeat; Slovakia eforms.uvo.gov.sk redirects to a Keycloak login wall at iam.uvo.gov.sk and opendata.uvo.gov.sk fails DNS resolution; Cyprus eprocurement.gov.cy is CAPTCHA-gated (not attempted, per policy); Austria gv.vergabeportal.at (ANKO) is an AngularJS SPA. 4) Carried from the 24/07/2026 vendors run and NOT retested this unit because search was down: mellon.hr and mellon.com.pl wp-json return non-JSON behind a WAF even with a desktop UA; mellongroup.com/news is JS-only with no underlying API; ir.euronetworldwide.com/gcs-web times out at 60s; the Bank of Greece and Central Bank of Cyprus EMI/PI registers need a desktop-browser fetch. 5) No source was dropped without being logged: every retained signal cites a URL that Agent 2 recorded as actually opened, and rows where only a portal register index (not a deep link) is available are cited as such — jnportal.ujn.gov.rs/contracts for the Serbian NBS register rows and e-licitatie.ro list endpoints for the Romanian direct-award rows. |
